\title{Melanie-MASTERS (human masters) — Alekhine Defense}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}Every move below was produced by relational analogy to named master games (human and/or top-engine -- the exact source is named per move). Each figure shows our position with Melanie's move (a) and the top retrieved analog positions (b--d) with the master move played there; the caption names the exact source and the piece-role correspondence under $\mu$ -- cross-type maps (a rook playing a queen's role) are called out, as no material/eval engine explains a move that way.\end{abstract}

\caption{\textbf{Move 7, White: \texttt{O-O}.} (a) Our position; Melanie plays \texttt{O-O} (\texttt{e1g1}). (c) Retrieved from \texttt{lichess\_elite\_2024-09\_p0018\#3039} (ply 20) [structural distance $d$=67, QAP obj=33]: the master played \texttt{O-O}; under $\mu$ our \emph{Ke1} plays the role of that game's \emph{Ke1}; cross-type roles: our \emph{n}b6 as their \emph{B}c1, our \emph{b}c8 as their \emph{P}b2; transferred move \texttt{e1g1} \textbf{(endorses this move)}. (d) Retrieved from \texttt{lichess\_elite\_2024-09\_p0025\#1487} (ply 20) [structural distance $d$=67, QAP obj=33]: the master played \texttt{O-O}; under $\mu$ our \emph{Ke1} plays the role of that game's \emph{Ke1}; cross-type roles: our \emph{n}b6 as their \emph{B}c1, our \emph{b}c8 as their \emph{P}b2; transferred move \texttt{e1g1} \textbf{(endorses this move)}. Synthesis: 3 retrieved analogs transferred to \texttt{O-O} (summed QAP objective 146, the strongest correspondence among the candidate moves); Melanie committed it via the \texttt{search} selector.}
